ⓘ How to Read This Report

Compliance Status

Compliant  — no open issues and all waivers are active.
Conditionally Compliant  — no open or invalid issues, but one or more waivers are not yet active.
Non-Compliant  — has open issues (Analyze / Fix Planned) or invalid / missing / mismatched waivers.

Waiver Quality

A waivered issue is one whose KW status is Ignore, Defer, or Not a Problem. Waiver quality is determined by the waiver ID in the issue comment.

  • Open Issues and Invalid / Missing / Mismatched Waivers1 — contribute to Non-Compliant; rows highlighted red in the Raw Issues table.
  • Inactive Waivers2 — contribute to Conditionally Compliant; rows highlighted yellow in the Raw Issues table.
  • Active Waivers — waiver status is Deviation Active; no row highlighting (fully compliant).

1 Invalid = missing or invalid waiver ID   Mismatch = waiver checker code does not match the KW issue checker code
2 Inactive = waiver found but not yet active

Klocwork Query

The Klocwork Query field in the Configuration Summary shows the filter used to fetch issues from the Klocwork server. Klocwork queries follow a simple convention:

  • * — Wildcard: fetch all issues in the project with no filter applied.
  • +module:name — Include only issues belonging to the module named name. Multiple +module: terms narrow the scope to those modules only.
  • -module:name — Exclude issues belonging to the module named name from the results. Commonly used to skip test code, third-party libraries, or example projects that are not under the project's direct quality control.

Example: * -module:test -module:examples fetches all issues except those in the test and examples modules.

MCUSDK-16008JIRAMISRA C
SITSW-7629JIRANPD.FUNC.MUST: Waiver for false positivesDeviation ActiveStatic AnalysisPermittedNot ApplicableProjectKW tool shows false positives of this issue sometimes. This has to be analyzed case by case and deviated if it it false positive.
MCUSDK_SA_DR_001ManualA buffer overflow, or overrun, is an anomaly in which a program writing data to a buffer overruns the buffer's boundaries and overwrites adjacent memory. Typically, this problem occurs when a program is copying strings of characters to a buffer. C and C++ provide no built-in protection against accessing or overwriting data in any part of memory, and do not automatically check that data written to an array (the built-in buffer type for this language) is within the array's boundaries. The ABV.GENERAL checker is a generic checker that looks for array bounds violations — any access to an array element that is outside of the bounds of that array.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectABV.GENERAL
MCUSDK_SA_DR_002ManualThe INFINITE_LOOP checkers find instances of loops that have no exit. The checkers track the variables that control loop exit, flagging a defect if the conditions are invariant. When the validity of a condition doesn't change from one iteration to another, the loop never terminates. The INFINITE_LOOP.LOCAL checker finds infinite loops whose exit conditions are based on a local variable.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectINFINITE_LOOP.LOCAL
MCUSDK_SA_DR_003ManualAn attempt to access data using a null pointer causes a runtime error. When a program dereferences a pointer that is expected to be valid but turns out to be null, a null pointer dereference occurs. Null-pointer dereference defects often occur due to ineffective error handling or race conditions, and typically cause abnormal program termination. Before a pointer is dereferenced in C/C++ code, it must be checked to confirm that it is not equal to null. The NPD checkers look for instances in which a null or possibly null pointer is dereferenced. The NPD.CONST.CALL checker flags situations in which a null-pointer constant value might be passed to a function that dereferences it without checking it for null.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectNPD.CONST.CALL
MCUSDK_SA_DR_008ManualABV.STACK is an inter-procedural checker that looks for buffer overflow inside a called function. Arguments passed to the function control which elements of the buffer are accessed. This checker finds defects when the buffer is not passed to the function, but is usually a local variable inside the function or a global variable.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectABV.STACK
MCUSDK_SA_DR_009ManualAn attempt to access data using a null pointer causes a runtime error. When a program dereferences a pointer that is expected to be valid but turns out to be null, a null pointer dereference occurs. Null-pointer dereference defects often occur due to ineffective error handling or race conditions, and typically cause abnormal program termination. Before a pointer is dereferenced in C/C++ code, it must be checked to confirm that it is not equal to null. The NPD checkers look for instances in which a null or possibly null pointer is dereferenced. The NPD.FUNC.MUST checker flags situations in which a pointer value from a function call that might return null is subsequently dereferenced explicitly or passed to a function that dereferences it without checking it for null.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectNPD.FUNC.MUST, NPD.FUNC.MIGHT, NPD.FUNC.CALL.MIGHT
MCUSDK_SA_DR_010ManualThe UNINIT.STACK.MUST checker finds instances in which a local variable of a non-class type is read when it hasn't been initialized. The UNINIT.STACK.MIGHT checker finds instances in which a local variable of a non-class type is read when it may not have been initialized.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectUNINIT.STACK.MUST, UNINIT.STACK.MIGHT
MCUSDK_SA_DR_011ManualThe NUM.OVERFLOW.DF checker detects possible cases of numeric overflow or wraparound in an arithmetic operation.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectNUM.OVERFLOW.DF
MCUSDK_SA_DR_012ManualThe CXX.SUSPICIOUS_INDEX_CHECK checker finds defects when a suspicious index check is present before accessing an array at a specific index. The CXX.SUSPICIOUS_INDEX_CHECK .CALL checker finds defects when a suspicious index check is present before accessing an array in another function.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectCXX.SUSPICIOUS_INDEX_CHECK, CXX.SUSPICIOUS_INDEX_CHECK.CALL, CXX.SUSPICIOUS_INDEX_CHECK.ZERO
MCUSDK_SA_DR_013ManualThe ABV.NON_ARRAY checker finds defects when any non-array object is used as an array.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectABV.NON_ARRAY
MCUSDK_SA_DR_014ManualAn attempt to access data using a null pointer causes a runtime error. When a program dereferences a pointer that is expected to be valid but turns out to be null, a null pointer dereference occurs. Null-pointer dereference defects often occur due to ineffective error handling or race conditions, and typically cause abnormal program termination. Before a pointer is dereferenced in C/C++ code, it must be checked to confirm that it is not equal to null. The NPD checkers look for instances in which a null or possibly null pointer is dereferenced. The NPD.GEN.MIGHT checker flags situations in which a pointer that's been assigned a null constant value locally might subsequently be dereferenced explicitly or passed to a function that might dereference it without checking it for null. The NPD.GEN.MUST checker flags situations in which a pointer that's been assigned a null constant value is subsequently dereferenced explicitly or passed to a function that dereferences it without checking it for null.Deviation ActiveAllNo plan to fixNot ApplicableProjectNPD.GEN.MIGHT, NPD.GEN.MUST, NPD.GEN.CALL.MUST
